**It reported 10 blocks claimed by more than one module — but my `@renders` names were DERIVED FROM PROSE**, so `thread` appears under five modules that each describe "the thread" in their SCREENS. ⭐ **And §268 says there IS one thread — one `Conversation` per `Person` across every channel — so five modules feeding one component is the DESIGN, not a violation.**

# 13. ⭐⭐ D3 RESOLVED — AND TWO OF THE FIVE ARE NOT OWNERSHIP DISPUTES

**Reading each module's WHAT changed the question:**

| Table | ⭐ Recommendation | Why |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **`ai_calls`** | **`C-Ai` owns · `C-Billing` reads** | *C-Ai does the routing, arbitrage and waterfall — **it makes the call and knows what it cost.** C-Billing meters from the row* |
| **`churn_scores`** | **`X-08` owns · `X-07` reads** | *X-08 IS the churn scorer — "it observes, it has no send." X-07 is MRR prediction and consumes the score* |
| **`form_submissions`** | **`X-155` owns** | *X-155 is the form RUNTIME for every site and widget.* ⛔ **X-109 submits INTO a prospect's OWN form — outbound — so it should not own this table at all; it needs its own** |
| ⛔⛔ **`memberships`** | **A TITLE COLLISION, not a dispute** | *X-165 = recurring SERVICE PLANS. X-192 = **chamber and directory memberships.*** ⭐ **Two different words spelled the same.** X-165 keeps `memberships`; X-192 takes `directory_memberships` |
| ⛔⛔ **`sync_conflicts`** | **A TITLE COLLISION** | *X-171 = OFFLINE DEVICE sync in a basement with no signal. X-173 = two-way ACCOUNTING sync to Xero/QuickBooks.* ⭐ **`device_sync_conflicts` and `accounting_sync_conflicts`** |

⭐⭐⭐ **Two of the five were never ownership questions — they are the TITLE-LIE pattern the plan names repeatedly** *(Plaid Sync read as bank verification · Multi-Entity Nesting read as agency hierarchy · Attendance Tracking read as staff attendance)*. ⛔ **A shared table name between two modules that mean different things is worse than a dispute: both are right, and the schema silently merges two datasets.**

# 20. ⭐ HOW THE DOCUMENTS ARE ORGANISED FOR CODING
> ⛔ **An agent never reads the master plan.** *`P-209`: the module contract is a FILE, and turn 86 assembles a brief FROM THE ANNOTATIONS AND FROM NOTHING ELSE.*

| Tier | File | Who reads it |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| ⭐ **1 · the entry point** | `GOAIEZ-START-HERE.md` | **the next window, first, always** — state, next turn, the verification rule |
| ⭐⭐ **2 · the open-items record** | **`GOAIEZ-AUDIT-LEDGER.md`** | **this file** — every open item, its owner, the estimate and why it moved |
| **3 · the source of truth** | `GOAIEZ-MASTER-PLAN.md` §1–§298 | the conductor · ⛔ **never an agent** |
| **4 · the generated inputs** | `TRACKER-MODULES` *(roster)* · `TRACKER-CAPABILITIES` *(887 rows)* · `LAW-SWEEP` *(the law binding — D4)* | ⭐ **`doctor`, `map`, `context`, `impact`** read these |
| **5 · what the agent gets** | ⭐⭐⭐ **the BRIEF** — generated by `brief <module>` from the header + spec rows | **the swarm agent, and nothing else** |
| **6 · the build rules** | `BUILD-PLAN` *(order, waves, batching, DoD)* · `TESTING-PLAN` *(the ⑤ roll-up, runtime proofs)* | the conductor and the harness |
| **7 · the runway** | `runtime/` — Procfile · supervisor · `app:deploy-check` · the runtime test group · `ci.yml` | ⛔ **the box** |
| **8 · reference** | `REGISTER` · `905-ASSIGNED` · `G1-AUDIT` · `THIRD-PARTY` · `DIGEST` · `SESSION-BRIEF` · `TURNS` | lookup only |

⛔⛔ **THE CHAIN THAT MATTERS: header → `doctor` → `brief` → agent.** *The plan never touches the agent, so a plan that is wrong is caught by `doctor` before it becomes a brief — **and that is the only reason 2.9 MB of documentation is safe to build from.***

# 26. ⭐⭐⭐ PHASE 1 · TURN 1·2 — RESOLVED, AND THE SILENT CLASS WAS REAL

## ⛔⛔ THE SILENT COLLISION I PREDICTED — IT EXISTS, AND IT IS THE WORST KIND
> **`C-Agent` CONFIRMS `business.greeting` with the tenant.**
> ⛔⛔ **`C-Sms` and `X-66` each INFER their own greeting "from the industry profile".**
> ⭐⭐⭐ **So the tenant confirms a greeting — and the text channel and the voice channel each use a DIFFERENT one they guessed.** *Nobody is asked twice, so nobody notices.*

**`X-66` was doing it three times over:** ⛔ it inferred **the greeting** *(C-Agent owns)*, **hours** *(X-108 owns)* **and the transfer target** *(C-Agent owns)* — **all three CONFIRMED by their owners, all three silently re-guessed by voice.**

## ⭐⭐ THE DISCRIMINATOR — 22 CANDIDATES → 4 REAL
| ⭐ **CORRECT — it NAMES its source** | *"the catalogue **from X-163**" · "the threshold **from C-Reviews**" · "the approval chain from **X-113's roles**"* — **these are READS and they say so** |
| :--- | :--- |
| ⛔ **WRONG — it names a generic source** | *"the greeting… **from the industry profile**"* for a Fact another module OWNS |
| ⚠️ **FALSE POSITIVE — same word, different thing** | `C-Telephony`'s **carrier** roster · `X-123`'s **DLQ** threshold · `X-204`'s **quiet** hours · `C-Sms`'s **send window** |
| ⭐ **THE GROUNDING PASS — correct by design** | `C-Agent` proposes hours and prices from the crawl; `X-163` seeds them **SAMPLE**; the owner confirms |

| **1 · 2** | `X-118` | ⛔⛔ **asks for NOTHING of its own now** — *`price.callout_fee` and `price.first_real_price` are `X-163`'s; the wizard **renders X-163's screen*** |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **3** | `X-153` | `business.hours` **READ from `X-108`** — *it already inferred "alert hours FROM business hours", so it was asking for the same Fact twice in one wizard* |
| **4** | `C-Sms` | `business.greeting` **READ from `C-Agent`** |
| **5 · 6** | `X-66` | **greeting · hours · transfer target — all three READ from their owners** |

⭐ **`§227.1` now holds: every Fact has exactly one asker, and every other reference names the owner it reads from.**

# 28. ⭐⭐⭐ PHASE 2 COMPLETE — THE AUTOPILOT INTENT FRAMEWORK · **1 turn**

## ⛔⛔ THE CHECK CAUGHT A DEFECT I CREATED THREE TURNS AGO
**Measured first: 52 of 119 ladders DISAGREED with their `@intent` day-one default — and 28 of those were `GROW` modules declared `L2`.** ⭐ *`§227.2`: **`GROW` ships `L1` — PROPOSES ONLY, ALWAYS, ON DAY ONE.***

⭐⭐⭐ **The ladders were MINE.** *I derived them from each module's AUTOPILOT prose — "runs continuously" → `L2 → L3` — and never checked them against `@intent`.*

## ⭐⭐ THE DIAGNOSIS: I CONFLATED TWO DIFFERENT NUMBERS
| **The prose describes** | what a module does **once trusted** — *"runs continuously"* |
| :--- | :--- |
| ⭐⭐⭐ **`§227.2` governs** | what it **SHIPS AT on day one** — *and that comes from `@intent`, not from prose* |

⛔ **A `GROW` module genuinely does run continuously at `L3` — once EARNED. It still SHIPS at `L1`.** *Both were true; my single-value ladder could only hold one of them.*

## ✅ THE FIX — THE LADDER IS NOW TWO VALUES
```
ships: L1 · ceiling: L3, EARNED
```
**`ships-at` is derived from `@intent` under `§227.2`. The ceiling is what the module may REACH on measured clean runs, never set.**

| ⭐ **ships-at agrees with `@intent`** | **100** |
| :--- | ---: |
| operator posture / `OBSERVE` — `n/a` | **19** |
| ⛔ **disagreeing** | **0** *(was 52)* |
| ⛔ **missing** | **0** |
| **TOTAL** | **119** |

⭐ **50 modules had their ships-at CHANGED by this pass** — *the 28 `GROW` modules that would have shipped acting instead of proposing are the ones that mattered.*

## ⭐ THE PHASE'S SCORECARD
**No estimate was given before it ran; it took ONE turn.** ⛔ *And it found that a field I had confidently reported as "119/119 complete" two turns ago was **wrong on 52 of them** — because completeness of a FIELD is not correctness of its VALUE.*

## ⭐ THE REFUSAL PATH CONNECTS — IT IS A REAL, DECLARED EDGE
**`X-126 @emits capability.decided` · `C-Agent @consumes capability.decided`.** ⭐⭐ *The gate's verdict reaches the agent through a DECLARED EVENT, not an import.* **`X-122` consumes it too — so every ACTION passes the same gate as every ANSWER.**

> **`C-Ai @consumes` read: *"every agent turn"*.**
⛔⛔ **A sentence, not a token.** *`doctor` cannot read it · `impact` cannot trace it · `map` cannot draw it.* ⭐⭐⭐ **The model layer's dependency on the agent — the busiest edge in the platform — was invisible to every tool in the programme.**
✅ **FIXED: `C-Ai @consumes agent.turn.started`** — *a token `X-148` already emits, never wired to `C-Ai`.*

## ⚠️ 277 OF 417 EVENTS HAVE NO NAMED CONSUMER — AND IT IS **NOT** A DEFECT
⭐ **`X-123 EventBus @consumes "everything — it is the transport"` and owns `event_log`.** *Every event is RECORDED whether or not a module subscribes.*
⭐⭐⭐ **`P-208` is DIRECTIONAL and the plan already says which way is dangerous:** *"every CONSUME needs an EMITTER, and a missing emitter is **invisible, silent**."*
| **emitted, no subscriber** | a RECORDED FACT waiting for a reader | **277** — ⭐ information, never a failure |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| ⛔ **consumed, no emitter** | **DEAD CODE that will never fire** | ⭐⭐ **2** — *the dangerous direction is clean* |
⛔ *A check that fails on 277 correct rows gets switched off by Friday.*

`X-121` *"nothing — the bottom of the graph"* · `X-123` *"everything — it is the transport"* · `X-124` *"every module's @provides via the registry"* · `X-125` *"every event on the bus"* · `X-193` *"every send.requested"* · `X-156` *"every inbound source"* · `X-128` ×2.

⛔ **Step 4 of the operating loop is `doctor module:done`. It did not exist.** *Turns 87–89 wrote its spec; **170 assertions sat across the package and zero of them ran.*** ⭐ **Now it is code.**

| **`BoundaryStage.php`** | cross-module imports · hardcoded model strings *(`P-194`)* · `env()` outside config *(`P-193`)* · wage fields *(`P-204`)* · ⛔⛔ **the forged-artifact check — `uniqid()` in a file that handles call/message/charge ids** · `default =>` on an enum |
| **`ContractStage.php`** | token format · duplicate `@module` anchors · `P-163` canonical nouns · **`P-208` in its DANGEROUS direction only** · two-emitter events · ⭐ **the PROSE rule with a NAMED exemption list** |
| **`ModuleDoneCommand.php`** | ⭐⭐⭐ **the seven gates, one boolean** |

## ⭐⭐ THREE DECISIONS INSIDE IT WORTH NAMING
**① The prose exemption is a NAMED LIST, not a pattern.** *`X-123` genuinely IS the transport, `X-121` genuinely is the bottom of the graph.* ⛔ **Exempting by name keeps the exemption visible and auditable; exempting by pattern would have re-admitted `C-Ai`'s "every agent turn".**

**② `P-208` fires in ONE direction.** ⛔ *consumed-with-no-emitter = **dead code that will never fire** → FAILS.* ⭐ *emitted-with-no-subscriber = **a recorded fact waiting for a reader** → **not reported as a failure at all**. 277 of 417 are in that state and they are correct.*

**③ `TESTED` rejects a proof that ran on the `sync` driver.** ⛔⛔ *A green suite proves nothing if the queue was synchronous — that is exactly how **M-95 stayed invisible for months while every test passed**.* ⭐ **And it requires an EXTERNAL ARTIFACT ID, because the swarm cannot fabricate one.**

## ⛔ WHAT `doctor` STILL NEEDS — 4 stages and a reader
**`SchemaStage` · `CapabilityStage` · `TestAnchorStage` · `JourneyStage` · `ManifestReader`.** ⭐ *The two built are the two that fail the COMMIT — **the cheapest and the most load-bearing***, and they are the ones every parallel agent hits first.

## ⭐⭐ FOUR DESIGN DECISIONS INSIDE IT
**① `ManifestReader` reads the CODE and nothing else.** ⛔ *Every stale-record defect this programme produced was a hand-maintained file — the status column, the side-file pointers, the unfixed `P-163` violation, the law grid claiming modules it never added, `@laws` written into 0 of 119 headers.* ⭐⭐⭐ **A document that can drift from the code is deleted and generated instead.**

**② `Manifest` carries `ships` AND `ceiling` as separate fields** — *because conflating them was a real defect: 52 of 119 disagreed with `§227.2`, **28 of them GROW modules that would have SHIPPED ACTING instead of proposing.*** ⭐ *A GROW module does run continuously at L3 — once EARNED. It still ships at L1. One field could only hold one of them.*

**③ The `sync`-driver rejection.** ⛔⛔ *Laravel runs queued jobs synchronously in tests — **exactly how "nothing runs the queue" stayed invisible for months while every test passed.*** ⭐ **A proof that ran on `sync` would pass with no worker at all, so it is not a proof.**

**④ Every violation carries its FIX.** *"add `@excludes X` to module Y", not "violation".* ⛔ **A check that only refuses gets disabled; one that proposes the fix gets maintained.**

# 36. ⭐⭐⭐ THE MISSING LINK — `module:scaffold`

## ⛔⛔ THE GAP NOBODY HAD NAMED
> **the chain is: header → `doctor` → `brief` → agent**

**The headers exist — 119 of them, in a 3 MB plan.** `doctor` reads `app/Modules/<id>/manifest.php`. ⛔⛔ **THERE ARE ZERO OF THOSE FILES.** *So every check passes vacuously, `brief` has nothing to assemble, and an agent would receive nothing.*

⭐ **`module:scaffold` generates them from the documented headers.** *Dry-run against the real plan: **119 scaffolded · 0 refused · 28 gaps reported.***

## ⭐⭐ WHY GENERATED AND NOT HAND-WRITTEN
**Every stale-record defect this programme produced was a hand-maintained file** — *the status column, the 57 side-file pointers, the `P-163` violation found and never applied, the law grid claiming seven modules it had not added, `@laws` in 0 of 119 headers.* ⭐⭐⭐ **A generated manifest cannot drift, because there is nowhere for it to drift to.**

⛔ **And it REFUSES rather than guessing.** *A header missing a required field produces a **reported gap**, never a filled-in default.* **`§298`: an annotation is DERIVED from what the thing does, never asserted to make it pass.** *28 gaps are reported and none is filled.*

## ⛔⛔ THE DRY RUN CAUGHT A BUG THAT WOULD HAVE CORRUPTED ALL 119
**Headers put several declarations on ONE line:** `` `@provides capability.check` · `@emits capability.decided` ``

⛔ **My first parse split at the next `@word` BEFORE stripping backticks — so the split never matched and `provides` swallowed the `@emits` behind it.** ⭐ *`X-126`'s manifest would have declared `capability.check · @emits capability.decided · capability.refused` as its PROVIDES list.*

| first attempt | **341 fields** carried a leaked declaration |
| :--- | :--- |
| my "fix" | ⛔ **338** — *it barely moved, and I measured instead of assuming* |
| ⭐ **strip markup FIRST, then split** | ⭐⭐ **0** |

⭐⭐⭐ **That is `N-262-05` in miniature: a check anchored on the wrong thing gives a confident, wrong answer — and only RE-MEASURING AFTER THE FIX catches it.** *Caught in a dry run, before 119 corrupt files existed.*

# 41. ⭐⭐⭐ THE TWELVE JOURNEYS — THE ONLY CHECKS THAT CROSS A SEAM

⛔ **Every other check in this programme is PER-MODULE.** *A module passing its own gate says nothing about whether a plumber can be called, quoted and booked.* ⭐ **`JourneyStage` named twelve and nothing produced their evidence. Now eight are written in full and four are declared, awaiting a dependency.**

## ⭐⭐⭐ JOURNEY ① IS NOT A TEST — IT IS THE PRODUCT
> ***"The missed call — the whole product in 60 seconds."***

**It measures, it does not claim.** ⛔ *If the number cannot be met, **THE PROMISE CHANGES, NOT THE MEASUREMENT.*** ⭐ *And it asserts the carrier's own message-id, because nothing in the system can mint one — plus that `ConsentService::decide()` was passed, since **a text-back to someone who has STOPped is the one message that must never send, and a missed call is exactly when a system is most tempted to skip the check.***

## ⭐⭐ THE ASSERTIONS THAT CARRY THE MOST WEIGHT
| ② **day one** | ⛔ *the agent answers a REAL call — **and must NOT quote**, because `X-163` owns the price and nothing is confirmed yet. An agent that invents a price on day one is the worst possible first impression* |
| :--- | :--- |
| ④ **STOP** | ⛔⛔ *not "no NEW steps are scheduled" — **nothing already QUEUED may fire.** A step enqueued before the STOP is the one that gets sent and the one that costs a complaint* |
| ⑤ **migration** | ⛔ *`P-203`. **Historical jobs look exactly like completed jobs, and a completed job triggers a review invite.** Importing a year of history would text 500 people about work finished last March — **and it looks like the system working correctly*** |
| ⑦ **agency isolation** | ⛔ *not "the UI hides it" — **the PAYLOAD must not contain it.** A field hidden by a template is one API call from a client who now knows their agency's markup* |
| ⑧ **restore** | ⭐⭐ ***a restore test that cannot FAIL is not a test, it is a ritual.*** *The backup is corrupted ON PURPOSE and verification must catch it* |

## ⭐ AND EVERY JOURNEY REFUSES THE SYNC DRIVER
**`assertQueueIsNotSync()` runs first in each.** ⛔ *Laravel runs queued jobs synchronously in tests — **a journey on `sync` would pass on a box with no worker at all**, which is precisely how "nothing runs the queue" stayed invisible for months while every test passed.*

## ⚠️ MY CROSS-CHECK OVER-MATCHED — ELEVENTH TIME, AND IT WAS IN THE CHECKER
*The slug comparison reported **"7 journeys expected but never written"**, including `fix`, `what` and `where`.* ⛔ **Those are the violation-array keys `'where' => … 'what' => … 'fix' => …` in `JourneyStage` itself.** ⭐ *Re-anchored on the `JOURNEYS` constant alone: **12 slugs, 12 covered, 0 missing.*** **The artifact was right; my check was wrong — for the eleventh time this session, and caught before it was reported as a defect.**

# 42. ⛔⛔⛔ R227 — THERE IS NO TIME GUARANTEE. THE OWNER HAS SAID THIS FIVE TIMES.

## WHAT I DID
**The plan says: *"the 60-second promise in our code instead of the phone company's."*** ⭐⭐⭐ **That sentence is about WHERE THE LOGIC LIVES — our text-back path rather than a carrier's forwarding rule. It was never a duration owed to anybody.**

⛔ **I built THREE LAYERS on it:**
| **①** | closed decision **D1** with *"60 seconds, already in the package — no conflict existed"* |
| :--- | :--- |
| **②** | wrote a journey test asserting `elapsed_ms < 60_000` **as a pass/fail** |
| **③** | listed *"nothing tests the sixty-second promise under load"* as gap **E1**, ⭐ **inventing a gap in a promise that did not exist** |

⭐⭐ *And I had already been told. **This is the second time this session I asserted something about the missed-call timing that the package does not say*** — the first was `R39`/`R81`/`R168`, three ruling numbers that appear in **zero** package files and which I raised eleven times.

## ✅ FIXED — EVERYWHERE
| **`R227` written as `§264F`** | ⛔ **no test, journey, gate or tenant-facing string asserts a duration.** `doctor` fails the build on any assertion comparing elapsed time to a constant |
| :--- | :--- |
| **journey ①** | renamed `a_missed_call_becomes_a_consented_text_back` — ⭐ *it asserts the text-back HAPPENED, was CONSENTED, and carries the CARRIER's own id* |
| **`JourneyStage`** | the 60-second threshold **deleted** |
| **`RuntimeProofTest`** | promise language removed |
| **`E1`** | reworded — ⭐ **the real gap is LOAD** *(concurrency, isolation, queue depth at 500 tenants)*, **not an SLA** |
| **all 27 code files** | ⭐ **0 duration assertions · 0 promise language** |
| **all package files** | **11 surviving mentions, every one now carrying `R227` inline** |

⭐ **Timing is still RECORDED in the evidence.** *A number in a log is information; the same number in an assertion is a promise nobody agreed to make.*

## ⭐⭐ AND THE 500 — I WAS RIGHT, AND BOTH NUMBERS ARE REAL
| ⭐ **`P-203`'s anchor, verbatim** | ***"import 500 completed jobs → assert ZERO outbound messages and zero `job.completed` subscribers fired"*** — **500 deliberately, because a 20-record import cannot show an event storm** |
| :--- | :--- |
| **the allowance** | **500 SMS/month, SHARED across missed-call · review · reactivation** |

⛔ *Two identical numbers meaning different things — **the `§241` collision pattern again**, and this time the journey used the right one.*

**Two false assertions about the same subject** *(`R39`/`R81`/`R168`, then the sixty-second promise)* **meant every other law I have cited this session needed checking.**

## ⭐ EXISTENCE — 30 LAWS, 30 PRESENT, 0 INVENTED
`P-060` `P-092` `P-128` `P-193` `P-194` `P-195` `P-198` `P-203` `P-206` `P-207` `P-209` `P-210` · `R34` `R130` `R184` `R193` `R197` `R199` `R200` `R209` `R211` `R219` · `LAW 128` `M-95` `N-262-05` `§150.4` `§227.1` `§227.2` `§259` `§298`.
⛔ **After `R39`/`R81`/`R168` this was not a formality — it was the check that would have caught them.**

## ⛔⛔ BUT EXISTING IS NOT SAYING WHAT I BUILT ON IT — AND ONE FAILED
**I read the six most load-bearing verbatim. Five matched exactly. `R34` did not.**

| ⛔ **what I built** | *`assertFalse($flow['retention_offer_shown'])` — **"a retention offer was shown during cancellation"*** |
| :--- | :--- |
| ⭐⭐⭐ **what `R34` SAYS** | ***"R34 RECONCILED: a save-offer may add NO STEP — one screen, both choices, cancel always one tap."*** |

⭐⭐ **The offer is ALLOWED. What is forbidden is a STEP.** ⛔ **My assertion was stricter than the law and would have FAILED A CORRECT IMPLEMENTATION** — *the same error as the sixty-second one, in the same direction: **I hardened a reconciled, nuanced law into an absolute.***

✅ **Fixed:** the assertion is now SCREEN COUNT *(the thing that cannot be argued about)*, and **if an offer is shown, both choices must be on that one screen and cancel must still be one tap.**

## ⭐ THE LESSON, WRITTEN DOWN
> ⛔⛔ **CITING A LAW IS NOT READING IT.** *Every law I cited existed. **One of six spot-read said something materially different from what I had built.*** ⭐ **A grep proves a law exists; only reading it proves what it says.**

| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| 6 read verbatim | **5 matched · 1 did not** | ⛔ `R34` |
| `R34` corrected in code | screen count, not offer presence | ✅ |
| duration assertions | **0** | ✅ |

## ⛔ SECOND FINDING — `P-163` SAYS **TWELVE**; MY CONSTANT HELD **THIRTEEN**

**`P-163` says *"the twelve canonical nouns"* — in FOUR places. `X-121`'s `@owns_table` lists THIRTEEN.** ⭐ *My constant matched `X-121` exactly, so a naive check said clean.*

⭐⭐⭐ **Both are correct, and the difference is real: `entity_history` is the AUDIT TRAIL OF the nouns, not a noun.** *`X-121` owns it for the same reason it owns the graph, but it is not a business entity anyone models.*

⛔ **Keeping them in one flat list quietly redefines `P-163` to mean thirteen** — *and the next person to count finds a law and a constant that disagree.* **That is exactly how "the 905" and the `CLASSIFIED` status column both started.**

✅ **Fixed in two files:** `CANONICAL_NOUNS` is now **twelve**; `X121_OWNED_NON_NOUNS` holds `entity_history`. **Both refuse the same claim — with DIFFERENT messages, naming the right law**, because a violation message that names the wrong reason is not fixable.

## ⭐⭐ THE AUDIT'S SCORE

| :--- | ---: | :--- | :--- |

| ⛔ **built on wrongly** | **2** | `R34` *(offer allowed, step forbidden)* · `P-163` *(twelve, not thirteen)* | both fixed |

> ⛔⛔ **CITING A LAW IS NOT READING IT.** *Every law existed. **Two of twelve said something materially different from what I had built on them** — and in both cases my version was STRICTER than the law, which is the failure mode that looks responsible and breaks correct implementations.*

# 45. ⛔⛔⛔ THE AUDIT CONTINUED — AND THE FOURTH FINDING IS THE WORST

**I read 12 and found 2 wrong. Rather than assume the rate dropped, I read 9 more. Two more were wrong.**

## ⛔ ③ `P-198` — I CITED THE WRONG LAW ENTIRELY
| **I wrote** | *`N-206-02`: "a credential reveal is named, time-boxed and logged **(P-198)**"* |
| :--- | :--- |
| ⭐ **`P-198` actually says** | ***"THE SECURE FIELD. ANY field on ANY entity may be marked `secure` — sensitive data arrives on four different entities, so a vault module would sit half empty while the real data stayed in notes."*** |

⛔ **A different law about a different thing.** ⭐ **The real rule is `§178`, and it is sharper than what I wrote:**
> ⭐⭐⭐ ***"REVEAL IS SCOPED BY OWNERSHIP — you may reveal what you own, you may never reveal what you do not."***
> ⛔⛔ ***"A secret is NEVER returned in plaintext to any caller — including its owner, including an admin, including the AI."***

✅ **`N-206-02` re-sourced.** ⚠️ *My version would have permitted a plaintext return to an owner, which §178 forbids outright.*

## ⛔⛔⛔ ④ `P-209` SAYS **SIXTEEN** ANNOTATIONS. MY READER PARSES **THIRTEEN**.
**And `P-209` warns about exactly this, in its own words:**
> ***"THE BACKFILL GATE: every module must declare every applicable marker BEFORE the Blueprint may run. Today `@intent` appears only where it was invented, `@sink` 8 times, `@reads_table` twice — a generator would produce 116 briefs in which arbitration, the day-one default and the ladder floor are simply absent."***

⭐⭐⭐ **A reader that silently parses 13 of 16 produces precisely that brief AND REPORTS SUCCESS.**

**The three missing that matter** *(`@laws` is correctly absent — struck by `D4`)*:
| `@sink` | where data LEAVES the system |
| :--- | :--- |
| ⛔⛔ **`@agent_reachable`** | ⭐⭐⭐ **THE ALLOW-LIST — and `P-209` says why: *"a deny-list FAILS OPEN: an action added in six months is reachable by the agent BY DEFAULT."*** |
| `@excludes` | a module naming a fenced term to say it does NOT do it |

⛔⛔ **Until `@agent_reachable` is parsed, nothing enforces the allow-list — and the fail-open case `P-209` warns about is the LIVE BEHAVIOUR.** ✅ *Named in `MISSING_ANNOTATIONS` so the gap is visible rather than silent; **the parser is not pretending to be complete.***

| :--- | ---: | :--- | ---: |

# 47. ⭐⭐⭐ `CitationStage` — THE MANUAL AUDIT, MADE MECHANICAL

**Six bad citations in thirty was a CLASS of error, not six instances.** ⭐ *Stage ⑦, ~3s, **fails the COMMIT.*** *It extracts every `P-` `R-` `M-` `N-` `LAW` reference from comments and human-facing messages and resolves each against the package.*

## ⛔⛔⛔ AND ITS OWN NEGATIVE CONTROL CAUGHT IT PASSING `M-95`
**First version: a citation resolves if the id appears anywhere in a source document. Run against `M-95` — the id that started all of this — ⭐⭐ IT PASSED.**

> ⛔⛔⛔ **Because writing the audit finding into the master plan HAD PUT `M-95` IN THE SOURCE.**

⭐⭐⭐ **Documenting a bad citation made the check accept it.** *A programme that writes about its own defects **will always contaminate the corpus it checks against** — so "appears somewhere" can never be the test.*

## ⭐⭐ THE FIX: DEFINITION **POSITION**, NOT PRESENCE
| `\| **P-163** \| …` | a table row |
| :--- | :--- |
| `**R34** — TEXT` | an em-dash statement |
| `**R219** WE DO NOT GATE` | a capitalised ruling |

⭐ **A mention is not a definition.**

**Measured over 24 known cases, iterated three times:**
| first attempt *(appears in a source doc)* | ⛔ **M-95 PASSED** |
| :--- | :--- |
| second *(definition position, 3 source files)* | 13/18 — ⛔ *5 real R-rulings live in the owner-decision files* |
| ⭐⭐ **third — definition position, whole package** | ⭐⭐⭐ **23 / 24 · phantoms refused 4/4 · real laws passed 19/20** |

⭐ **All four phantoms refused: `M-95` · `R39` · `R81` · `R168`** — *the exact ids I cited 4 and 11 times respectively.*

## ⚠️ WHAT THE STAGE CANNOT DO — AND IT SAYS SO IN ITS OWN DOCBLOCK
⛔ **It proves a law EXISTS. It cannot prove the code MATCHES it.** *Five of the six findings were of the second kind — `R34` forbids a STEP not the offer, `R193` names THREE things, `P-163` says TWELVE, `P-198` is a different law, `P-209` names SIXTEEN annotations.* ⭐⭐ **Every one cited a real law and described it wrongly. No grep catches that. Only reading does.**

# ⭐ 31 runtime files · `doctor` now has **SEVEN** stages

⭐⭐ *A fix applied to one clause of a field is not a fix to the field.*

**`X-208` · `X-212` · `X-214` · `X-215` all read `ships: L2 · ceiling: L1 (fixed)`.** ⭐⭐⭐ **A ceiling BELOW the ships-at is not a ladder — it is a contradiction, and it means the module SHIPS ABOVE ITS OWN CEILING.**

⛔⛔ **`X-214` would have shipped surcharging at `L2` — ACTING — when `R209` says `L1 FOREVER`.** *A surcharge applied automatically to a customer's card on day one.*

**Cause: my bulk rewrite derived `ships` from `@intent` alone and never checked it against the module's own `L1 FOREVER`.** ⭐ **Ruling: the specific rule beats the general one — an explicit `L1 FOREVER` in a module's own law BEATS `§227.2`'s intent default.** ✅ *All four now `ships: L1 · ceiling: L1 (fixed)`.*

**I replaced `C-Ai`'s prose `@consumes "every agent turn"` with the token `agent.turn.started`.** ⛔ **Nothing emitted it.** *`C-Ai` and `X-148` both subscribed to an event that did not exist* — ⭐ ***"a subscriber to nothing is dead code," P-208, in the direction the plan itself calls dangerous.***

# 64. ⭐⭐⭐ R233 — THE SCOPE EDGE SETTLED: `AGENCY` AND `GOAIEZ` ARE DIFFERENT **KINDS**

⛔⛔ **The problem:** *an AGENCY sees its clients' data **by design**; GO AI EZ must **not** see tenants' data.* **Same column, opposite rules — and both look like "a higher scope reading a lower one," so a naïve `issuer_scope` check either permits both or forbids both. Each is catastrophic in a different direction.**

| ⭐ **`AGENCY` = DELEGATED** | reads a client's rows **because the client granted it** — an explicit, revocable **grant ROW**. ⛔ *The grant is DATA, not hierarchy: remove the row, access is gone that instant* |
| :--- | :--- |
| ⭐⭐ **`GOAIEZ` = PEER** | **tenant #0.** Reads its own rows and nothing else — *the same rights a plumber has.* ⛔⛔ **NOT above tenants. BESIDE them** |

⭐⭐⭐ **So "a different access level" was one word off: `AGENCY` is a different LEVEL; `GOAIEZ` is a different TENANT.**

**Enforced in `SchemaStage`, not in prose:**
| ⛔⛔ **`N-233-02`** | `agency_client_grants` **must carry `CHECK (grantee_scope <> 'GOAIEZ')`.** *Without it, **ONE INSERT makes Go AI EZ a superuser over every tenant** — no code change, no review, no deploy.* ⭐ **A CHECK constraint is the only thing that survives an operator with database access** |
| :--- | :--- |
| **`N-232-03`** | `doctor` fails any file reading `issuer_scope` from the request — *a scope the caller can name is a scope the caller can change* |
| **`N-233-03`** | a revoked grant bites on the **next READ**, not the next login — *the realistic breach is a warm session, not a forged token* |

# 66. ⭐⭐⭐ `impact` AND `map` — AND THE DRY RUN CAUGHT ME POLLUTING THE GRAPH

**`§263`'s read commands. Two of four built, and only buildable NOW** — *`impact` needs a complete graph, and `P-208`'s dangerous direction only reached ZERO this session. **Before that the graph had 37 holes and `impact` would have been confidently wrong 37 ways.***

## ⭐⭐ `impact` — `N-263-02` IS ITS ENTIRE DESIGN
> ***"impact is exhaustive or it REFUSES — a partial impact answer reads as 'nothing else is affected'."***

⛔⛔ **That inverts the normal instinct.** *A search that finds 3 of 5 callers is mildly useful; **an IMPACT that finds 3 of 5 is WORSE THAN NOTHING**, because the two it missed read as "safe to change" and someone ships.* ⭐ **So it runs a COMPLETENESS GATE first and refuses with the specific hole named, rather than answering over a broken graph.**

## ⭐ `map` — `N-263-03`: GENERATED EVERY RUN, STORED NEVER
> *"a stale map is the roster problem again"* — ⛔ **and the roster problem was REAL: 122 modules in the plan, 119 in both trackers, nothing noticed.** ⭐⭐ *`map` writes nothing to disk. It cannot be committed, edited, quoted from memory, or disagree with the code — **because it IS the code, rendered.***

# 67. ⛔⛔⛔ AND THE DRY RUN FOUND MY OWN NOTES CORRUPTING THE PARSE

**`impact send.requested` reported TWELVE emitters. I had declared EIGHT.**

⭐⭐⭐ **The cause was my own writing.** *When I declared `approval.requested`, I explained it as **"the `send.requested` shape, applied to approvals"** — and that note sits INSIDE the `@emits` line:*
> `@emits` `` `approval.requested` `` ⭐ *(2026-08-27 — the **`send.requested`** shape, applied…)*

⛔⛔ **Four modules — `X-103` `X-145` `X-190` `X-205` — were credited with emitting an event they do not emit, because the parser read my EXPLANATION as a DECLARATION.**

## ⭐⭐ WHY THIS MATTERED MORE THAN A BAD DRY RUN
**`module:scaffold` uses the same parse to generate all 122 `manifest.php` files.** ⛔ *The pollution would have been **baked into the manifests**, and every downstream tool — `doctor`, `brief`, `impact`, `map` — would have agreed with each other and all been wrong together.*

✅ **Fixed in the generator, five ordered steps:** *stop at the next declaration → **drop parenthetical NOTES** → drop strike commentary → strip markup → drop trailing prose.* ⭐ **Measured: 12 → 8.**

## ⚠️ THE PATTERN, AND IT IS NOW UNMISTAKABLE
> ⛔⛔ **This is the FOURTEENTH over-match of the session, and the third caused by prose living inside a declaration line.**
> ⭐⭐⭐ *`N-262-05` says "no check anchored on markup." **The deeper rule this session has taught: a declaration line must contain ONLY declarations. Commentary belongs on its own line** — because every parser that has ever read these headers has eventually mistaken a note for a fact.*

# ⭐ VERIFIED AFTER THE FIX
| modules parsed | **122** | `send.requested` emitters | ⭐ **8, correct** |
| :--- | ---: | :--- | ---: |
| `approval.requested` emitters | **5, correct** | ⛔ **`P-208` orphans** | ⭐⭐ **ZERO** |

# 68. ⭐⭐⭐ ONE PARSER, AND THE FIELD THAT CONTAINED A PARAGRAPH

**Finding #67 fixed the scaffold's parser. But EVERY tool had its own regex — which is exactly how they diverged.** ⭐ **`DeclarationParser` is now the single one: `doctor`, `brief`, `map`, `impact` and both scaffolds read through it.** ⛔ *A bug found by one is fixed for all five at the same moment.*

## ⭐ MEASURED EXPOSURE
| declaration lines | **892** | carrying a note | **339** |
| :--- | ---: | :--- | ---: |
| ⛔ **notes containing a token a parser reads as REAL** | **35** | modules affected | **29** |

⛔⛔ **`@owns_table` is the dangerous field, not `@emits`** — *a table name is any lowercase word.* ⭐⭐⭐ **`C-Sms`'s note reads *"messages themselves live on X-121's `messages`"* — a naive parser records `C-Sms` as OWNING `messages`, one of `P-163`'s twelve canonical nouns, ON THE STRENGTH OF THE SENTENCE THAT DENIES IT.**

## ⛔⛔⛔ AND ONE FIELD WAS NOT A DECLARATION AT ALL
**`X-103`'s `@renders` held an entire SWARM paragraph:** *"on-page contract for X-143 · must not touch the template library after fork, the Fact store (it emits `site.published`)"*

⭐⭐ **So `@renders` had NO declared value — and a parser read `site.published` out of the prose as a RENDERED BLOCK.** ✅ *Repaired to `@renders none`; the guidance moved to `**SWARM**` where it belongs.*

## ⭐ NOW ENFORCED — SHAPE, NOT LENGTH
**`doctor` fails the COMMIT on prose in a declaration field.** *Every item must look like a token or be the literal `none`.* ⭐ *Length was the wrong test — it flags `X-121`'s twelve canonical nouns (a legitimately long list) and my own owner-confirmed provenance notes (verbose but correctly stripped).* ⛔ **Shape is the right test, and it catches exactly the paragraph case.**

## ⚠️ AND MY NARRATIVE DETECTOR OVER-FLAGGED — FIFTEENTH TIME
*It flagged `X-215` and `X-212` as corrupted.* ⛔ **They are my own owner-confirmed notes, which the parser already strips correctly.** ⭐ *Checked before acting: **the only real defect in twelve flagged lines was `X-103`.***

# 69. ⭐⭐⭐ THE FIFTEEN GAPS — NINE WERE PROSE QUANTIFIERS, AND FOUR SPINE MODULES READ AS "CONSUMES NOTHING"

**The scaffold had reported "16 field gaps" for turns without anyone opening them.** ⭐ *Opened: **15 empty declaration fields across 14 modules**, and they were not oversights.*

## ⛔ NINE WERE PROSE QUANTIFIERS
*`X-123` `@consumes` read **"everything — it is the transport."*** ⭐⭐⭐ **True, and unparseable.** *`X-121` said "nothing — the bottom of the graph"; `X-125` "every event on the bus"; `X-01` "every inbound event".*

⛔⛔ **A prose quantifier parses to an EMPTY field, so the four SPINE modules — `X-123` EventBus, `X-125` flows, `X-207` push, `X-156` ingest — read as CONSUMING NOTHING.** ⭐ ***The exact opposite of the truth, for the four modules that consume the most.***

| ⭐ **the fix** | **`@consumes *` — the WILDCARD, DECLARED** |
| :--- | :--- |
| ⛔ **and fenced** | *legal for the four spine modules **only, NAMED not patterned*** — everything else must name its events |
| `X-121` | → `none` *(it really is the bottom of the graph — but written as a declaration)* |
| `X-01` | → must NAME its events; **the wildcard is not available to it** |

## ⛔⛔ AND MY OWN PARSER ATE THE WILDCARD — TWICE
**First version stripped `*` as markup: the wildcard parsed EMPTY and the spine modules still read as consuming nothing.** *Second attempt used a lookaround and still ate it.* ⭐⭐⭐ **What worked, verified against the real headers before shipping: PROTECT the backticked wildcard, strip everything, restore it.** *Sequencing beats cleverness — the same lesson as step ①.*

## ⭐ AND FOUR FIELDS HAD PROSE SWALLOW THEM — THE `X-103` DEFECT, FOUR MORE TIMES
`X-143` · `X-116` · `X-117` `@renders` and `X-119` `@owns_table` — *all held SWARM paragraphs instead of declarations.* ⛔ **`X-119` was the worst: it opened with "none for *" so it parsed as owning NOTHING, while three real tables sat buried after the note.**

## ⚠️ SIXTEENTH OVER-MATCH — AND THIS ONE MANUFACTURED WORK
**My verification used `@owns_table\s+`. A declaration may open with a backtick and NO space.** ⛔ *So the checker reported `X-119` as empty **after I had already fixed it correctly**.* ⭐⭐ **The artifact was right and the checker was wrong — the harder failure to notice, because it invents work rather than hiding it.** *Recorded in `DeclarationParser` so no future caller repeats it.*

# 70. ⭐⭐⭐ THE LAST FOUR COMMANDS — `Q-089` IS COMPLETE

> **`Q-089`: *"the four commands are the checks, so no check is ever a prompt again:* `doctor` · `map` · `context` · `impact` *(+* `find` · `make:module` · `why` · `brief`*)."*** ⭐ **All eight now exist.**

## ⭐⭐⭐ `context` — AND ITS PROOF IS SHARPER THAN THE LAW ASKED FOR
> **`N-263-01`: *"context NEVER returns another module's brief — proven by asking for X-180 and diffing against X-186* **(THE COLLISION THAT ACTUALLY HAPPENED)**."*

⛔⛔ **That parenthesis is why the defence is STRUCTURAL, not diligent.** *One manifest, loaded by EXACT id, and every printed line reads from that one object.* ⛔ **No fuzzy match, no "did you mean", no related-modules section — because a helpful near-miss is precisely how `X-180` became `X-186`.**

⭐⭐⭐ **The simulation found something worse than the law describes:**
| ask `X-18` | exact match → ⛔ **REFUSED** · fuzzy → ⭐⭐ **`X-188`** — *a THIRD, unrelated module* |
| :--- | :--- |

*The collision was never just 180-vs-186. **A substring matcher hands back whatever it reaches first**, and an agent then builds the right code against the wrong contract — compiling cleanly, passing its tests, implementing something nobody asked for.*

## ⭐ `find` — `N-263-04`: DECLARATIONS AND CODE, **NEVER PROSE**
> *"the plan describes things never built and things later killed"*

⛔ **This programme has the receipts:** *`Payroll Export` survived in prose long after `P-204` fenced payroll · a Miami shoe-shop example kept reappearing in a product for plumbers · `X-168` advertised "overtime math" through eight gates.* ⭐⭐ **Searching prose returns things that were killed. Searching declarations returns what the system IS.** *`--prose` exists, and labels its results **"NOT evidence that anything exists."***

## ⭐ `why` — IT REFUSES RATHER THAN INVENTING A REASON
**Answers from a DEFINITION POSITION, never a mention** — *the `CitationStage` lesson.* ⛔ **If no reason is recorded it says so:** *"`M-95` is mentioned 4 times and DEFINED 0 — three of those were the author quoting themselves."* ⭐⭐⭐ **An invented rationale is worse than a missing one, because it ends the enquiry.**

## ⭐⭐ `make:module` — THE LOCK IS THE POINT
**`N-264-05`: refuses a header with fewer than eight fields** *(measured: all 122 would pass)*. ⛔⛔ **And it takes the lock BEFORE reading the ceiling** — *reading then locking is the same race with extra steps.* ⭐ *Wave 0 builds three modules in parallel; **without the lock, three agents read the same ceiling and mint the SAME id three times.*** *This programme already lived the un-locked version: **the roster drifted to 122 while both trackers said 119.*** ⭐ **It reads the ceiling from the MANIFESTS, never a tracker — a tracker is a claim, the manifests are the thing.**

| commands | **12, zero duplicate signatures** | ⛔ unresolvable injections | ⭐ **0** |
| stages implementing `run(): array` | ⭐ **7 / 7** | | |

⭐⭐ **The one unresolved import is `RuntimeProofTest` → `App\Jobs\SendMissedCallTextBackJob`, and it SHOULD be unresolved** — *that test exists to assert the job is missing. **A test that compiles cleanly here would mean the runtime layer was already installed**, which it is not.*

## ⚠️ AND THE CHECKER LIED TWICE MORE — 17th AND 18th
| ⛔ *"12 unresolved imports"* | **11 were phantom.** *My class-detector matched the word `class` inside a COMMENT — `ManifestReader`'s docblock says "the class parses…", so the tool read its class name as `parses`* |
| :--- | :--- |
| ⛔ *"`App\Doctor\Manifest` unresolved"* | **False.** *It is declared `final readonly class Manifest` and my pattern did not allow `readonly`* |

⭐⭐⭐ **Both fixed by anchoring on LINE START and allowing the real modifiers.** *`N-262-05` again — and the tally is now **eighteen** over-matches this session, **every one caught before it drove a change**.*

# 72. ⭐⭐ `N-263-01` IS NOW EXECUTABLE, NOT JUST WRITTEN
**`install-step-0.sh` gained a step 8:** *it runs `map`, `map --events`, `map --tables`, `find`, `context`, `why` and `impact` once each against REAL data* — ⛔ **because a command that loads but crashes on its first real input is indistinguishable from one that works, until an agent needs it at 3am.**

⭐⭐⭐ **And it asserts the collision law as a TEST:**
> `php artisan context X-18` **must FAIL.** *If it succeeds, the installer ABORTS with: "context resolved a PARTIAL id. That is how X-180 once returned X-186."*

⭐ *The law stops being a sentence in a document and becomes a thing the install refuses to complete without.*

# 74. ⭐⭐⭐ `INSTRUCTIONS.jsonl` — APPEND-ONLY, HASH-CHAINED, `reason` MANDATORY

**The plan: *"INSTRUCTIONS.jsonl + `why` — append-only, queryable, SURVIVES BOTH MODELS."*** ⭐⭐ *That last clause is the point: a decision made in one conversation, by one model, must still be answerable months later by a model that was never in the room.*

## ⛔⛔ WHY IT IS HASH-CHAINED — THIS PROGRAMME'S OWN RECEIPTS
| a `P-163` violation | found, written down, **never fixed** |
| :--- | :--- |
| ⛔⛔ **§235** | **claimed 41 changes applied — measurement found ZERO** |
| `X-168` | carried a struck capability **through eight gates** |
| the roster | **drifted to 122 while both trackers said 119** |

⭐⭐⭐ **Every one was A CLAIM THAT OUTLIVED ITS EVIDENCE.** *A hash chain makes that specific failure impossible: **you cannot edit an entry without breaking every entry after it**, so "it says here we did X" becomes checkable rather than trusted.*

**Proven by simulation:** *editing an entry's `reason` and rehashing it → **"line 2: prev-hash mismatch — entry edited or removed."*** ⭐ **The tamper is detected at the NEXT entry, not the edited one.**

⛔ **There is no `update()` and no `delete()`, deliberately.** *Append-only is not a policy that can be followed carelessly — **it is the absence of any other method.*** ⭐ *And `reason` throws if empty: a constraint whose reason is lost gets removed by the next competent person, because it looks arbitrary.*

## ⭐ CHECK 11 IS NOW REAL
**`doctor:module-done` verifies the chain, then appends a `verified` line.** ⛔⛔ **Without it, DONE means somebody said so.** *With it, DONE means there is a hash-chained, reason-carrying entry.* ⭐ **And if the chain is broken, the gate REFUSES to record DONE at all.**

# 75. ⭐⭐ THE TWELVE JOURNEYS — 12 WRITTEN, **0 SKIPPED**
**The last four were `markTestIncomplete()`, and that was the wrong call.** ⛔⛔ *A skipped test is invisible in a green run; **a FAILING test names what is missing every single time the suite runs.*** ⭐ *The runtime proofs already worked this way on purpose — "a green suite that proves nothing is worse than a red one that proves something."*

| ⑨ **invoice-to-paid** | ⛔ *asserts the GATEWAY's own charge id — **"marked paid with no charge id" means no money moved*** |
| :--- | :--- |
| ⑩ **review-invite** | ⭐⭐ *asserts ONCE — **then completes a SECOND job and asserts still ONE.** Asking twice is how a tenant's number gets reported* |
| ⑪ **site-publish** | *all seven: pixel · chat · form capture · DNI · SEO · schema · SSL* |
| ⑫ **dunning-by-reason** | ⛔ *`R211`: **resolution PRECEDES any automatic stop** — and no dunning action fires without a recorded reason* |

# ⭐⭐⭐ CHAT WORK IS COMPLETE
**41 runtime files · 12 commands · 7 stages · 12 journeys · 122 modules · 799 rows · 0 deviations.**
⛔ **Remaining chat turns drop to ~4–8** *(Step-0 fallout, and re-verifying the ten `@consumes` against real code).* ⭐ **Everything else is build turns, and they do not happen here.**

# 76. ⛔⛔⛔ THE PRE-FLIGHT — AND IT FOUND THE MOST DANGEROUS GAP IN THE RUNTIME

**Rather than declare the chat work done, I checked what would actually FAIL when `install-step-0.sh` runs.** ⭐ *Three broken artisan calls — and one of them was not a typo.*

## ⛔⛔⛔ `db:bootstrap` DID NOT EXIST — AND `SchemaStage` DEPENDS ON IT
**`SchemaStage` asserts every tenant table has RLS `FORCE`. NOTHING created the roles or applied it.** ⭐⭐⭐ **So the check would have run against a database where RLS was never enabled, found nothing to complain about, AND REPORTED CLEAN.**

⛔⛔ **Why `FORCE` is not optional, stated plainly:**
> **Postgres applies row-level security to ordinary roles but NOT to the table OWNER and NOT to a superuser — and Laravel usually connects AS THE OWNER.**
> ⭐ *So a policy can be written perfectly, reviewed, hand-tested — **and bypassed completely by the exact connection the application uses.***

✅ **Built.** *Creates `goaiez_app` / `goaiez_owner` **as separate roles** (an owner bypasses RLS, so the app must connect as somebody who cannot) · `ENABLE` + **`FORCE`** + a `tenant_isolation` policy on every table carrying `tenant_id` · and **`R233`'s `CHECK (grantee_scope <> 'GOAIEZ')`**.*
⭐ *Idempotent, and the installer now **HARD-FAILS** on it instead of warning: **"do not continue — the tenant boundary is not enforced."***

⭐⭐ **And the policy reads the scope from the SESSION, never a parameter** — `R232 N-232-03`: *a scope the caller can name is a scope the caller can change.*

## ⭐ THE OTHER TWO
| `pixel:publish` | **a command that was never built**, called by `DeployCheckCommand` → *repointed at `doctor --stage=schema`* |
| :--- | :--- |
| *"doctor green on all six stages"* | **stale by one** — `CitationStage` made it seven → corrected |

# ⭐⭐⭐ PRE-FLIGHT CLEAN
| ⛔ broken artisan calls | ⭐ **0** | ⛔ undeclared flags | ⭐ **0** |
| :--- | ---: | :--- | ---: |
| php files | **28, 0 malformed** | commands | **13** |
| `doctor` stages | **7** | unresolved imports | ⭐ **1 — and it is CORRECT** |

⭐ *`BUILD-STATE.json` and its lock are both guarded — `fopen(…,'c')` creates the lock, the state read is `is_file`-checked.* ⛔ **Every file path the code assumes is either a package file that exists, or one the scaffold creates.**

⭐⭐ **Step-0 fallout estimate drops from 3–6 turns to 1–3** — *the predictable failures are now fixed; what remains is genuine first-contact with the live tree.*

## ⭐⭐ AND THE FIX HAD A TRAP IN IT
**The obvious repair is to stub the thirty so the suite runs.** ⛔⛔⛔ **That is far worse than the bug.**

✅ **`JourneyHarness` — every one of the thirty THROWS a named exception saying what it must do against the REAL transport:**
| `waitForOutbound` | *"poll for an outbound row carrying the **provider message id**"* — ⛔ *not a queued job, not an intent* |

| `corruptBackup` | *"corrupt the backup **ON PURPOSE** so verification has something to catch"* |
| `walkCancelFlow` | *"**COUNT SCREENS** — screen count is the thing that cannot be argued about"* *(`R34`)* |
| `signUp` | *"exactly two fields — **a third is a `P-207` violation**"* |

⛔ **The installer now says it out loud: *"Do NOT stub them to get green."***

# 78. ⛔⛔⛔ NOTHING WAS REGISTERED — THE BUNDLE LARAVEL COULD NOT SEE

**I checked the one thing that would make NOTHING run.**

> ⛔⛔⛔ **13 commands · 7 `doctor` stages · a hash-chained instruction log · 12 journeys — and NOTHING registered them with Laravel.**

⭐⭐⭐ **The failure would have been maximally expensive: `install-step-0.sh` runs composer, writes `.env`, migrates, bootstraps roles and RLS FORCE, scaffolds 122 manifests and 122 capability files — every step reporting SUCCESS — and then reaches `php artisan doctor` and is told THERE IS NO SUCH COMMAND.** *An hour of work, then nothing.*

## ⭐ WHY IT WAS INVISIBLE TO EVERY CHECK I RAN
*Brace-balance passed. Imports resolved. Static calls resolved. Signatures were unique. Stage wiring was clean.* ⛔⛔ **Every one of those checks asks "is this file correct?" — and not one asks "does anything LOAD it?"**

✅ **`GoaiezRuntimeServiceProvider`** — *all 13 commands registered, verified against what is on disk: **13 registered, 13 on disk, 0 missing, 0 unimported.***

⭐⭐ **And it binds `ManifestReader` as a SINGLETON — correctness, not performance.** *Eleven classes inject it. A fresh instance each time re-parses every manifest eleven times per command, **and two instances can disagree mid-run if a scaffold writes between them** — the exact drift shape this programme keeps finding.*

## ⭐⭐⭐ THE INSTALLER NOW REGISTERS IT — AND PROVES IT WORKED
| **Laravel 11+** | writes into `bootstrap/providers.php` |
| :--- | :--- |
| **Laravel 10** | detects `config/app.php` and says exactly what to add — ⭐ *the live tree's version is not ours to assume* |
| ⭐ **then** | `composer dump-autoload -o` — *new classes are invisible files without it* |
| ⛔⛔ **then it PROVES it** | `php artisan list \| grep doctor` — ***and DIES if the command is not there*** |

> ⭐ **Trusting the grep on the config file would have been "claimed but never applied" for the sixth time.** *The installer asks ARTISAN, not the file it just edited.*

# ⭐ FINAL — 44 FILES
**30 php · 0 malformed · 30 classes/traits · 13 commands · 7 stages · 0 broken artisan calls · 0 undefined helpers · 1 unresolved import that is CORRECT.**

⭐⭐ **The pattern across findings 76, 77 and 78 is one thing:** *I verified that each file was RIGHT, three times, and never asked whether the files were **CONNECTED** — to the database, to each other, or to the framework.* ⛔ **Correctness in isolation is not a system.**

# 79. ⛔⛔ CI HAD THE IDENTICAL DEFECT — AND I ONLY LOOKED BECAUSE OF FINDING 78

**I fixed registration in the installer. `ci.yml` calls `artisan doctor` four times and registered NOTHING.**

⭐⭐⭐ **Every CI run would have failed with "command not found"** — *after composer, after migrate, after `db:bootstrap`, with all of it green.*

⛔ **`ci.yml` was never re-verified after I added the test groups** — *it lives in outputs and not in my working copy, so it fell outside every sweep.* ⭐ **The lesson from 78 is what sent me to look: I had checked whether files were CORRECT, never whether they were CONNECTED.**

✅ **Fixed identically in both pipelines** — *register → `composer dump-autoload -o` → ⭐⭐ **ask ARTISAN whether the command exists, and fail loudly if not.*** ⛔ *Trusting the file we just wrote is how "claimed but never applied" happens; **both pipelines now ask the framework instead.***

⛔ *Twenty-one over-matches this session. **The last four were all my checker reading COMMENTS as code** — including comments I had just written to explain the very fix being checked.*

# 90. ⛔⛔⛔ THERE IS NO DNC GATE — AND `R229` INVENTED ONE

> **Owner: *"there is no DNC gate — only DNC marked, or unsubscribe, or third-party scrubbing API OFF BY DEFAULT."***

**My `R229` had written: *"② third-party REGISTRY integration — a DNC-registry scrub before any outbound."*** ⛔⛔ **That is a MANDATORY GATE, and `P-066` already forbade it — verbatim, in the plan, the whole time:**

> ***"The compliance registers (DNC · litigator · reassigned) are provider slots, DEFAULT OFF, the tenant's own credentials, and BLOCK NOTHING UNLESS ENABLED (R64/R65); NOT ENABLED is indistinguishable from never existing."***

⭐⭐⭐ **And `P-066`'s own spec says the failure runs the OTHER WAY:** *"**a register that is OFF silently delays or blocks a send**" is the DEFECT · an enabled provider that times out **falls through to the rest of the decision, never to a block** · `dnc_listed` refuses **only on a real positive**.*

⛔ **My version would have made every tenant's outbound depend on a registry they never configured** — *and it would have been read as the compliance-conscious choice.*

## ⭐ WHAT ACTUALLY SUPPRESSES
| ① | a record **MARKED `dnc`** |
| :--- | :--- |
| ② | an **UNSUBSCRIBE / opt-out request** — immediate |
| ③ | ⭐ a **third-party scrubbing API** — an OPTIONAL integration, tenant's own credential, ⛔ **DEFAULT OFF** |

## ⛔ AND THE CORPUS CARRIES THE SAME WRONG CLAIM IN FOUR PLACES
*`905-ASSIGNED` and the god-tier register both say **"Before any campaign sends, the list is pinged against the National Do Not Call registry"** — the exact shape `P-066` forbids.* ✅ **All four marked** *(corpus text, not law — `§0.1`)*, and **the ledger's own stale `R229` summary superseded.**

## ⭐⭐⭐ THE FOURTH TIME — AND NOW IT IS A NAMED PATTERN
| `R34` | *the offer is ALLOWED; only the STEP is forbidden* |
| :--- | :--- |
| `R193` | *the export is THREE things, not two* |
| `P-163` | *TWELVE nouns, not thirteen* |
| ⛔ **`R229`** | ***no gate — provider slots, default off*** |

> ⛔⛔ **I harden nuanced rules into absolutes. It looks responsible and it breaks correct implementations.** ⭐ *Three of the four were caught by reading the law; **this one was caught by the owner**, which means reading is not sufficient — **the law has to be read BEFORE the ruling is written, not after.***

⭐ **`R65` is now CONFIRMED rather than reconstructed** — *`P-066` states it verbatim, and the owner has confirmed it.*

## ⛔⛔ `R228` CONTRADICTED `P-144` — AND THE OWNER'S ORDER IS WHAT RESOLVES IT
> **`P-144` read: *"CAPTCHA is solved ONLY in `X-109` form outreach, **on the TENANT'S own key**."***
> **`R228` extended it to Go AI EZ's internal marketing, on GO AI's key.**

⭐ *The owner ORDERED that extension — **"captcha is tenant and go ai internal marketing system"** — so `R228` is right.* ⛔⛔ **But `P-144` still read as an ABSOLUTE, so the package carried two laws that disagreed.**

✅ **`P-144` AMENDED IN PLACE**, not left to be discovered: *"solved ONLY in form outreach, **on the OPERATOR'S OWN KEY**"* — with `R228` named, the old wording quoted, and ⭐⭐ **everything else preserved intact: the solve lives on the SUBMIT of a form being legitimately pitched, NEVER on the READ, and the no-Turnstile-bypass-for-scraping fence stands.**

## ⭐⭐⭐ THE LESSON, SHARPENED
> ⛔ **`R229` was me making a law STRICTER. `R228` was me making one LOOSER — and BOTH left the package self-contradicting.**

⭐ *The error was never the direction. **It was writing a ruling without amending the law it touched.*** *A ruling that quietly overrides a law leaves two absolutes in one corpus, and the next reader obeys whichever they find first.*

**Every one of the seven now either agrees with its domain's law or explicitly amends it, in place, with the prior wording preserved.**

*It reported `P-195` "NOT FOUND" — a law I cited all session.* ⛔ **Its definition row spans lines and my single-line pattern could not see it.** ⭐ *Re-read with a working pattern: **"THE AUTONOMY LADDER. Every AI action sits at one of four levels and MOVES…"** — present, and exactly what I built on.*

# 93. ⭐⭐⭐ THE SELF-CONTAMINATION BUG IS NOW FIXED IN THE CHECK, NOT IN MY METHOD

**It bit twice — `M-95`, then the twenty rulings — so it is a defect in `CitationStage`, not in how I search.** ✅ **Two changes:**

| ⛔⛔ **① SELF-EXCLUSION** | *the corpus now EXCLUDES `AUDIT-LEDGER`, `LEGACY-RULING-INDEX` and `COMPLETION-ESTIMATE` — **the three files this programme writes ABOUT ITSELF***. ⭐ **Definition-POSITION alone was never enough, because a ledger table row IS a definition position** |
| :--- | :--- |
| ⭐⭐⭐ **② CARRIER AWARENESS** | *an id is also defined when a **P-law row's BODY cites it** — `\| P-011 \| Trials need no card **(R31)**…`* **The P-law holds the content; the R-id is its PROVENANCE.** *Fifteen of twenty resolve this way and the old stage called every one of them missing* |

**Real laws PASSED 8/8:** *`R31` and `R68` by position · ⭐ **`R65` via carrier `P-066`** · `R87` via carrier `P-148` · `P-066` · `P-144` · `R229` · `R231`.*

⛔ *I had asked the owner: **"'and for a window afterwards' implies a duration and no length is specified."*** ⭐⭐ **There is no duration because there is NO TIMED WINDOW — the latch holds UNTIL EXPLICITLY RELEASED.** *A stronger rule than a timer, needing no decision at all.* ⚠️ **The "window afterwards" wording came from a looser SECOND mention; the definition row is unambiguous.**

# 94. ⭐⭐⭐⭐ R234 — THE FOUNDING PREMISE. **THE AI IS ALWAYS ON.**

> **Owner: *"It's the other way around. If AI can't handle it, a human takes over. **AI is always on, handling everything, unless turned off by the client on certain things.** AI and autopilot is the WHOLE PURPOSE of the platform — humans use it so they DON'T have to do anything, or as little as possible. The platform runs their business AND generates business for them."***

⛔⛔⛔ **`R21` was written backwards and I had just quoted it approvingly.** *It read "a human takes over → the AI produces nothing until released", as though **human control is the resting state and AI action is the interruption.*** ⭐⭐⭐ **The truth is the inverse: the AI is the DEFAULT OPERATOR; a human is the EXCEPTION PATH.**

| ⛔ **NOT** | *"the AI proposes and a human approves"* — **that is a tenant doing work** |
| :--- | :--- |
| ⭐⭐⭐ **BUT** | ***the AI ACTS; a human is notified, can undo, can switch a lane off*** |
| ⭐⭐ **`R21`, corrected** | *the latch is **SCOPED TO ONE THREAD** and is an exception — **it never pauses the agent, campaigns, SEO, review asks or outreach*** |

## ⭐ `P-096` ALREADY SAID IT — WHAT WAS MISSING WAS ITS **STATUS**
> *"Automatic by default — replies, agent, review asks, posts, sends, digests, top-ups, renewals — with ONE per-tenant 'Review first' toggle per lane."*

⭐⭐ **The law existed as one of two hundred. `R234` makes it THE PREMISE every other decision is measured against.**

## ⭐⭐⭐ AND I TESTED THE PLATFORM AGAINST IT RATHER THAN ASSUMING
**56 modules ship at `L1` PROPOSE. If `L1` means "the tenant approves", that is `R234` violated 56 times.** *So I measured whether they RISE:*
| ⭐ **48 of 56 promote** *(ceiling `L2`/`L3`, EARNED)* | the ladder works — **`L1` is a starting point, not a resting place** |
| :--- | :--- |
| ⭐⭐ **8 stay at `L1` FOREVER — and every one is named** | `X-214` surcharge · `X-215` signatures · `X-211` AR · `X-212` migration · `X-208` physical mail · `X-203` backups · `X-210` promotion creation · `X-127` the platform's own posture |

⛔ **Money, migration, signatures, backups and irreversibility.** ⭐⭐⭐ **`P-096` already carves exactly those out: "the assistant ASKS on anything IRREVERSIBLE."** *No violation — the platform already holds to `R234`.*

## ⭐ MADE PERMANENT, THREE WAYS
| **`§264N`** | the ruling, with `N-234-01`→`05` |
| :--- | :--- |
| ⭐⭐ **`START-HERE`** | **`R234` is now the FIRST THING ANY READER SEES**, above the state block |
| ⭐⭐⭐ **`doctor`** | **fails a module that ships `L1` with no ceiling AND no named reason** — *"a tenant approving output forever is a tenant doing work forever"* |

> ⛔⛔ **`N-234-05`, the measure of every feature: WORK REMOVED FROM THE TENANT.** *A feature that adds a screen the tenant must visit daily has failed, however well it works.*

# 102. ⭐⭐⭐⭐ R236 — **THE ONLY LEGITIMATE OFF STATE IS "NOT YET SET UP"**

> **Owner: *"Not all should be on with autopilot **unless it has not yet been set up**."***

⭐⭐ **This is not an exception to `R235` — it names the ONE honest reason a lane can be dark, and forbids every other.**

| ⭐ **`ON`** | the default; **nothing was switched on to get here** |
| :--- | :--- |
| ⭐⭐ **`NOT_CONFIGURED`** | ⛔ *no number, no credential, no connected profile —* **A CAPABILITY PROBLEM, NEVER A PERMISSION ONE** |
| ⭐ **`OFF_BY_CLIENT`** | their choice, their switch, one tap back |

⛔⛔⛔ **No fourth state. `AWAITING_APPROVAL` · `PENDING_ENABLE` · `PROPOSE_ONLY` · `AWAITING_REVIEW` · `PENDING_APPROVAL` — `doctor` FAILS on all five**, because *a fourth state is a permission gate wearing a new name.*

## ⭐⭐ AND `NOT_CONFIGURED` IS A JOB FOR THE AI, NOT A SCREEN
| `N-236-02` | *the assistant asks for **THE ONE MISSING THING, in conversation, when it needs it** — "I can start posting to your Google profile — connect it here?"* ⛔ **never fourteen empty fields** |
| :--- | :--- |
| `N-236-03` | ⭐⭐⭐ **a lane dark for more than one cycle RAISES** *(`R219`)* — ***a lane silently dark for a month is the failure this platform exists to prevent*** |
| `N-236-05` | *one lane waiting for a credential **never blocks the others** — the same rule as `R21`'s latch* |

⚠️ **The risk I am naming: `NOT_CONFIGURED` becoming the new `L1`.** *A team under pressure will mark a lane "not configured" rather than turn it on.* ⛔ **`N-236-03` is the guard and it is the assertion most likely to be quietly dropped.**
⭐ **The boundary I applied: if it CAN act at all, it is `ON`. `NOT_CONFIGURED` means PHYSICALLY IMPOSSIBLE, not SUBOPTIMAL.**

# 104. ⛔⛔⛔ THE CODE STILL BELIEVED §227.2 — `brief` WOULD HAVE REFUSED **EVERY MODULE**

**`R235` raised all 122 ladders in the PLAN. The RUNTIME still enforced the rule it overruled.**

> **`Manifest::shipsAtByLaw()` returned §227.2's mapping — `SERVE`/`INFORM` → `L2`, `RECOVER`/`GROW` → `L1` — and `BriefCommand` REFUSED any module whose `ships` disagreed with it.**

⭐⭐⭐ **Every module now ships at its CEILING. So that four-line helper disagreed with ALL 122 AT ONCE, and `brief` would have refused every single one.** ⛔⛔ **The entire platform unbriefable — because a helper still believed a struck rule.**

*And it would have looked like a data problem, not a law problem: 122 identical refusals, each naming §227.2, a section the owner had already overruled.*

## ✅ FIXED IN FOUR FILES
| `Manifest::shipsAtByLaw()` | ⭐ **now returns the CEILING** — *"there is no separate 'the law says' answer left to disagree with"* |
| :--- | :--- |
| `BriefCommand` | *refuses on `ships ≠ ceiling`, **not** on the intent default* |
| `ModuleScaffoldCommand` | *the generated manifest comment taught "GROW ships L1, PROPOSES ONLY, ALWAYS" — **it would have written that into 122 files*** |
| `WhyCommand` | *answered "it ships at X because §227.2 derives the day-one level"* — **the wrong reason, to whoever asked why** |

## ⛔ AND THE `Manifest` CLASS DOCBLOCK TAUGHT THE OLD MODEL
*"ships = what it starts at on DAY ONE, from @intent under §227.2 · ceiling = what it may REACH on measured clean runs, **never set**… A GROW module genuinely does run continuously at L3 — **once EARNED. It still ships at L1.**"*
⭐ **Rewritten.** *The two fields survive because a module still HAS a ladder position — **but the position DESCRIBES the action, it does not gate when it may run.***

# 108. ⭐⭐⭐ THE `doctor` LOOP — THE RUNBOOK

**The owner: *"how do we do this loop to get to zero?"*** ⭐ **`GOAIEZ-THE-DOCTOR-LOOP.md`.**

## ⭐ WHY IT TERMINATES AND MINE DOES NOT
**Every violation carries `{where · what · fix}` — the SHAPE is fixed by the `Stage` interface.** ⭐⭐⭐ *The `fix` field is not advice, it is the instruction: **written when the check was written, by someone who knew the failure mode.***

## ⭐⭐ SEVERITY ORDER — ALREADY IN THE CODE, NOT A PREFERENCE
| `boundary` · `contract` · `citation` | ⛔⛔ **COMMIT** — *fix FIRST; a broken contract poisons every parallel branch* |
| :--- | :--- |
| `schema` · `capability` | ⛔ **MERGE** |
| `journey` | **WAVE** — ⭐ *fix LAST; **a journey fails BECAUSE of an open contract violation**, and chasing it first fixes the same thing twice* |

## ⛔⛔⛔ THE THREE WAYS TO CHEAT — NAMED, BECAUSE THEY ARE THE FAST PATH
| **delete the assertion** | *the id goes missing and **the requirement is gone*** — `P-210` |
| :--- | :--- |
| **stub the harness** | ***12 journeys PASS while touching no carrier, no gateway, no queue*** |
| **widen an `@ingress` list** | *the check is silenced and the bug stays* — `§235`'s lists are **NAMED, never patterned**, exactly so this shows in a diff |

> ⭐⭐⭐ **The test for any fix: DID THE SYSTEM CHANGE, OR DID THE CHECK CHANGE?**

## ⭐ AND THE STOP RULE THAT MATTERS MOST
> ⛔⛔ **If the count does not fall after a fix, STOP. The fix did not land.**
*That is not a rare failure — **it happened five times while this package was written**, every time as an edit reporting success and changing nothing.*

## ⚠️ THE HONEST CAVEAT ON THE RUNBOOK
**Every `fix` string was written against DOCUMENTS. None has been tested against a failing case.** ⛔ *If they turn out unhelpful at scale, the loop gets much slower and I need to know early — **the fix text is the entire reason this loop does not need me.***

⭐⭐ **The installer now ENDS by handing over the loop** — the four commands, the stop rule, and the three cheats, printed where the person is standing when it finishes.

## ⛔⛔⛔ THE COUNT WENT UP — TWICE — AND THAT IS THE SYSTEM WORKING
| pass | count | what happened |
| :--- | ---: | :--- |
| 1 | **7** | two `@owns_table` claims + five undefined citations |
| 2 | **4** | ⭐ *fixed; the count FELL* |
| 3 | ⛔⛔ **28** | ***my parser fix was WRONG*** — I truncated at the FIRST backtick, which is the OPENING one, **emptying every declaration** |
| 4 | ⛔ **9** | *second guess — skip one tick, stop at the next — **over-read the per-token shape*** |
| 5 | **5** | ⭐ the real rule found |
| 6 | ⭐⭐⭐⭐ **0** | |

> ⭐⭐⭐ **The stop rule I wrote in the runbook fired on me, twice, within minutes of writing it.** *"If the count did not fall, STOP — your fix did not land." **It did not land, and the number said so before I could believe otherwise.***

## ⭐⭐ WHY BOTH GUESSES FAILED — THERE ARE **TWO** DECLARATION SHAPES
| `` `@owns_table a · b` `` | **one pair around the whole list** |
| :--- | :--- |
| `` `@owns_table` `a` · `b` `` | ⭐ **a pair around EVERY token** |

⛔⛔ **No backtick rule can serve both.** ⭐⭐⭐ **What works: PROSE ANNOUNCES ITSELF** — *it begins at an italic note `*(`, a `⭐`/`⛔`/`⚠️` glyph, or a spaced em-dash, **never mid-token.*** ✅ *`DeclarationParser` now splits there, and **both wrong guesses are recorded in the file** so the next person does not repeat them.*

# 116. ⛔⛔⛔ *"THIS FILE IS NOT IN YOUR LIST — `GOAIEZ-*.md`"* — THE OWNER CAUGHT A REAL DEFECT

**`place-files.sh` assumed it was sitting INSIDE a `runtime/` folder with the `.md` files one level up:**

```bash
PKG="$(cd "$SRC/.." && pwd)"     # ⛔ the folder holding GOAIEZ-*.md
```

⛔⛔⛔ **Downloads from a chat arrive FLAT.** *One directory. `BriefCommand.php` next to `GOAIEZ-MASTER-PLAN.md` next to `TwelveJourneysTest.php`, **no subdirectories at all.*** ⭐⭐ **The script would have failed on its first real use** — *`$SRC/..` would point at `~/Downloads`, and `find` would either miss everything or copy the whole Downloads folder into the Laravel root.*

| :--- | :--- |
| ⭐⭐ | **works whether the folder is FLAT, nested, or a mix** — *it `find`s each name wherever it is* |
| ⭐ | **`ci.yml` handled separately** — *the ONE filename shipped twice; every other name in the package is unique, which is what makes the map safe* |

**116 files dumped into a single flat directory, then placed into a bare tree:**
*76 package files · 13 commands · 8 stages · 4 doctor core · 1 provider · 4 tests* — ⭐⭐⭐ **and 0 namespace/path mismatches, so PSR-4 autoloads every one.**

# 118. ⛔⛔⛔ `private function table()` — **ONE METHOD TOOK DOWN EVERY ARTISAN COMMAND**

> **`Access level to App\Console\Commands\ImpactCommand::table() must be public (as in class Illuminate\Console\Command)`**

**`Illuminate\Console\Command` already defines a PUBLIC `table()` for rendering console tables. I declared `private function table()` — and PHP refuses to LOAD such a class at all.**

⭐⭐⭐ **It did not fail in `impact`. It killed `package:discover`, so EVERY artisan command on the tree died — including `composer install` itself.** ⛔ *One private method in one command took the whole platform down.*

## ⭐⭐ AND IT WAS UNFINDABLE BY TESTING THE COMMAND
**This is a fatal at CLASS LOAD, not at call time.** *No amount of running `impact` would surface it, because the class never loads.* ⛔ **Every static check I ran — braces, imports, signatures, namespaces — passes on a file that PHP will refuse to load.**

## ✅ FIXED, AND THEN MADE UNREPEATABLE
| ⭐ | **`table()` → `tableImpact()`**, method + call site |
| :--- | :--- |
| ⭐⭐ | **I scanned all 13 commands against Laravel's 37-method base API** — *`table` `line` `info` `error` `ask` `confirm` `choice` `call` `option` `argument` `output` `fail` `trap` …* — ⭐ **exactly 1 collision, now 0** |
| ⭐⭐⭐ | **`BoundaryStage` now FAILS the build on any `private`/`protected` override of a base-class method**, with the reason: *"PHP refuses to LOAD the class otherwise, which kills package:discover and every artisan command on the tree — not just this one"* |

# 119. ⭐ THE 8 PSR-4 WARNINGS ARE **NOT MINE** — AND ONE IS INTERESTING
**All eight live in `app/Services/**`, a directory this package never writes to.** *They are `Skipping` WARNINGS, not the fatal — two separate problems in one output.*

| ⛔ **no namespace at all** | `SupportDeskMacros` · `VoiceAgentBuild2` · `MarketingDemoDoors` · `DemandIndex` · `LanguagePackLoader` · `AdminHealthMonitor` · `OpsAlerts` |
| :--- | :--- |
| ⭐⭐ **namespace CASE mismatch** | ***`App\Services\OAuth\OAuthManager` in `Services/Oauth/`*** |

> ⭐⭐⭐ **The second one is worth knowing about: PSR-4 is CASE-SENSITIVE.** *`OAuth` ≠ `Oauth`, so that class is **invisible to the autoloader on Linux and works fine on macOS** — a bug that only appears when you deploy.*

⚠️ **`doctor --stage=boundary` will report all eight once it runs.** *They are pre-existing tree defects, not regressions — **and they are exactly the kind of thing this package exists to surface.***

| `BoundaryStage.php: hardcodes 'gpt-4'` · `'claude-3'` · `'gemini-1'` … | ⭐⭐⭐ **those strings ARE the needles it hunts for** |

Twenty legacy rulings "resolved" because the ledger listing them became the evidence.* ⭐⭐⭐ **Both were caught by hand. This is the first time it appeared in CODE — and the shape is identical: A TOOL THAT DESCRIBES A DEFECT WILL MATCH ITS OWN DESCRIPTION.**

✅ **`app/Doctor` excluded — the checker cannot check itself.** ⭐ *`app/Console/Commands` is deliberately NOT excluded: a command that hardcodes a model string is a real violation, and commands carry no pattern lists.*

## ⚠️ TWO MORE FALSE POSITIVES, EACH A DIFFERENT FLAW
| ⛔ **`ImpactCommand: match() carries a default arm`** | ⭐⭐⭐ ***`match (true)` IS NOT AN ENUM MATCH.*** *It is a chained conditional and its `default` is the mandatory else branch. **Flagging it teaches people the checker is noise, which is how a REAL default arm later gets ignored.*** ✅ exempted |
| :--- | :--- |
| ⛔ **`BriefCommand: names a wage field 'withholding'`** | ⭐⭐ *`BriefCommand` holds the FENCED-TERM TABLE — `'withholding' => 'P-204'`. **It was flagged for containing the word it exists to ban.*** ⭐ *A rule and a violation of that rule are not the same thing.* ✅ fence files exempted |

## ⭐ WHAT IS GENUINELY REAL — AND IT IS IN THE LIVE TREE
| `Enums/AiModel.php` | **hardcodes `gpt-4` and `gpt-5`** — `P-194`: no model string lives in code |
| :--- | :--- |
| **10 enums + `RecordBrandRegistration`** | ***`match()` with a `default` arm*** — *the arm that silently absorbs the enum case added next week* |
| `Livewire/OmniAssistant.php` | **hardcodes `gpt-4` AND calls `env()` outside `config/`** — ⛔ *`env()` returns **null** once the config is cached, so that line works in dev and returns nothing in production* |

⭐⭐ **Those are pre-existing tree defects, found in 292ms by a stage that had never run.** *That is the package doing the job it was built for.*

> ⭐⭐⭐ **The honest summary: the checker's first act was to catch its own defect class, and its second was to find real bugs in a 2,786-file tree. Both are the system working.**

# 121. ⛔⛔ THE OWNER CAUGHT A **WRONG `fix` STRING** — THE EXACT THING I ASKED FOR

**My violation read:** *"hardcodes the model string 'gpt-4' → **read it from the `ai_models` table (P-194)**"*

⭐⭐⭐ **`ai_models` DOES exist — it is a declared WAVE-0 spine table.** ⛔⛔ **But a module never reads it.** *A module asks `C-Ai`, which resolves the row.*

> ⛔ **A fix string that names the wrong mechanism sends someone to write a direct query against a spine table — which is the `P-163` violation one layer down.** ⭐ *I warned that these strings were written against documents and never tested against a failing case. This is the first one to be tested, and it was wrong.*

✅ **Corrected:** *"`R237`: ask `C-Ai` — `$ai->for($moduleId, $jobClass, $slot)`. The row lives in `ai_module_assignments`; **the module never names a vendor and never queries `ai_models` directly.**"*

# 122. ⭐⭐⭐⭐ R237 — **EVERY MODEL, ANY VENDOR, ASSIGNED PER MODULE**

> **Owner: *"We never hardcode any AI. ALL models — Grok, Gemma, DeepSeek, ChatGPT, Claude, local self-hosted, anything we choose. And different models for different modules — primary and backup, and complex models for complex things."***

⭐⭐ **`P-194`'s OPEN ROSTER LAW already made the ROSTER a table.** *`R237` opens the **ASSIGNMENT** too.*

| ⭐ **PRIMARY** | what runs — cost and latency on the ordinary case |
| :--- | :--- |
| ⭐⭐ **BACKUP** | ⛔ **a different VENDOR, not a different model from the same one** — *a vendor outage takes every model it serves* |
| ⭐⭐⭐ **COMPLEX** | *the hard jobs.* **Three slots, not two, because "cheap by default, expensive when it matters" is the whole economics** — *one model per module either overpays on every trivial call or fails the hard ones* |

⭐ **New table `ai_module_assignments`** *(module · job_class · slot · model)*, owned by `C-Ai`. ⭐⭐ **A self-hosted Ollama or vLLM endpoint is a ROW like any other** — `self_hosted = true`, base url, no vendor key.

## ⭐⭐ AND THE CHECKER KNEW ONLY THREE VENDORS
**The literal list was `gpt-4 · gpt-5 · claude-3 · claude-4 · gemini-1/2/3`.** ⛔⛔ ***It would have passed a file hardcoding `grok-2`** — the enum it exists to kill just moves to a vendor it never heard of.*
✅ **Now a PREFIX list across 12 vendors** — *OpenAI · Anthropic · Google · **xAI · DeepSeek · Meta · Mistral · Alibaba · Microsoft · Cohere***. ⭐ *New models from a known vendor are caught automatically; only a genuinely new vendor needs a line.*

## ⚠️ THE TWO THINGS I DID NOT DECIDE FOR YOU
| ⛔ **who decides a job is "complex"** | *too eager and you overpay on everything; too shy and the hard jobs fail quietly.* **An owner decision** |
| :--- | :--- |
| ⚠️ **the COMPLEX slot could become a way to route everything to the expensive model** | *`C-Ai` already owns cost routing and token arbitrage — **the complex slot must sit UNDER that ceiling, not beside it***, or gap `E3` gets worse |

⭐ **And the owner's read on `Enums/AiModel.php` was right:** *it was probably written when there was one model and it was the only one.* ⛔ **`P-194` overruled that and `R237` finishes it — the file becomes a SEEDER for `ai_models`, or it goes.**

## ⭐⭐⭐ THE ONE THAT MATTERS MOST WAS ALREADY FOUND AND IGNORED
**`§177`: *"the OpenAI fallback needs a requested-vs-served column or every model comparison is invalid."*** ⛔⛔ **Nothing acted on it.** *Without `model_requested` AND `model_served`, a silent fallback attributes the BACKUP's output to the PRIMARY — **so every eval, every cost report and every "which model is better" decision is measuring the wrong thing.***

## ⭐⭐ THE RULE THAT MAKES `R237`'s OPEN ROSTER SAFE
> **`N-238-19`: a model may not enter a PRIMARY slot without passing that job class's golden set.**
⛔⛔⛔ *Otherwise **"any model, any vendor" means "any quality, discovered in production."***

## ⭐⭐ AND THE ATTACK SURFACE NOBODY HAD NAMED
**This platform reads SCRAPED SITES, INBOUND EMAIL, REVIEWS and UPLOADED FILES.** ⛔⛔ *Every one is attacker-controlled text that reaches a model.* ⭐⭐⭐ **A tool call originating from untrusted content is REFUSED — and logged as an ATTACK, not an error, because a rising count is an incident and nothing else in the system would show it.**

## ⚠️ THE TWO RISKS I NAMED RATHER THAN HID
| ⛔ **the ten-step path becoming ten seconds** | *on a 2-second classify call the overhead can exceed the work.* ⭐⭐ **Gate, budget and cache must be sub-10ms LOCAL lookups — if they are not, the core becomes the thing every module wants to bypass, and that is how the second entrance gets built** |
| :--- | :--- |
| ⚠️ **the redaction boundary** | *a phone number is easy; **a name in conversational context is not**, and over-redacting destroys the context the model needs.* ⭐ *Mandatory with a `self_hosted` exemption — **which makes self-hosting the honest answer for the most sensitive job classes***, but that is a cost decision for the owner |

**82 → 82, and the proof is in the text: the output still says *"read it from the `ai_models` table (P-194)"*.** ⭐⭐ **That string does not exist in my current `BoundaryStage` — it now says *"R237: ask C-Ai for the model."*** ⛔ *The old files are still in the tree; `goaiez-runtime.sh` was never re-downloaded.*
